<div class="col-sm order-1">
      <h2 class="chapter_title">CHAPTER 115B. ENVIRONMENTAL RESPONSE AND LIABILITY</h2>
      <h1 class="fgh">ENVIRONMENTAL RESPONSE AND LIABILITY ACT</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.115B.01">
        <h1 class="shn">115B.01 CITATION.</h1>
        <p>Sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115B.01">115B.01</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115B.20">115B.20</a> may be cited as the "Environmental Response and Liability Act."</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.115B.01.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1983/0/121/#laws.0.1.0">1983 c 121 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2004/0/228/#laws.1.73.0">2004 c 228 art 1 s 73</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.115B.02">
        <h1 class="shn">115B.02 DEFINITIONS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115B.02.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Application.</span></h2>
          <p>For the purposes of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115B.01">115B.01</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115B.20">115B.20</a>, the following terms have the meanings given them.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115B.02.1a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 1a.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/128/#laws.2.56.0">2003 c 128 art 2 s 56</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115B.02.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Act of God.</span></h2>
          <p>"Act of God" means an unanticipated grave natural disaster or other natural phenomenon of an exceptional, inevitable, and irresistible character, the effects of which could not have been prevented or avoided by the exercise of due care or foresight.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115B.02.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Agency.</span></h2>
          <p>"Agency" means the commissioner of agriculture for actions, duties, or authorities relating to agricultural chemicals, or for other substances, the Pollution Control Agency.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115B.02.3a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3a.<span class="headnote">Agricultural chemical.</span></h2>
          <p>"Agricultural chemical" has the meaning given in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/18D.01#stat.18D.01.3">18D.01, subdivision 3</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115B.02.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Commissioner.</span></h2>
          <p>"Commissioner" means the commissioner of agriculture for actions, duties, or authorities related to agricultural chemicals or the commissioner of the Pollution Control Agency for other substances.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115B.02.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Facility.</span></h2>
          <p>"Facility" means:</p>
          <p>(1) any building, structure, installation, equipment, pipe or pipeline (including any pipe into a sewer or publicly owned treatment works), well, pit, pond, lagoon, impoundment, ditch, landfill, storage container, motor vehicle, rolling stock, or aircraft;</p>
          <p>(2) any watercraft of any description, or other artificial contrivance used or capable of being used as a means of transportation on water; or</p>
          <p>(3) any site or area where a hazardous substance, or a pollutant or contaminant, has been deposited, stored, disposed of, or placed, or otherwise come to be located.</p>
          <p>Facility does not include any consumer product in consumer use.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115B.02.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Federal Superfund Act.</span></h2>
          <p>"Federal Superfund Act" means the Comprehensive Environmental Response, Compensation and Liability Act of 1980, United States Code, title 42, section 9601 et seq.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115B.02.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.</h2>
          <p>[Renumbered subd 1a]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115B.02.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Hazardous substance.</span></h2>
          <p>"Hazardous substance" means:</p>
          <p>(1) any commercial chemical designated pursuant to the Federal Water Pollution Control Act, under United States Code, title 33, section 1321(b)(2)(A);</p>
          <p>(2) any hazardous air pollutant listed pursuant to the Clean Air Act, under United States Code, title 42, section 7412; and</p>
          <p>(3) any hazardous waste.</p>
          <p>Hazardous substance does not include natural gas, natural gas liquids, liquefied natural gas, synthetic gas usable for fuel, or mixtures of such synthetic gas and natural gas, nor does it include petroleum, including crude oil or any fraction thereof which is not otherwise a hazardous waste.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115B.02.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">Hazardous waste.</span></h2>
          <p>"Hazardous waste" means:</p>
          <p>(1) any hazardous waste as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.06#stat.116.06.11">116.06, subdivision 11</a>, and any substance identified as a hazardous waste pursuant to rules adopted by the agency under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.07">116.07</a>; and</p>
          <p>(2) any hazardous waste as defined in the Resource Conservation and Recovery Act, under United States Code, title 42, section 6903, which is listed or has the characteristics identified under United States Code, title 42, section 6921, not including any hazardous waste the regulation of which has been suspended by act of Congress.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115B.02.9a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9a.<span class="headnote">Institutional controls.</span></h2>
          <p>"Institutional controls" means legally enforceable restrictions, conditions, or controls on the use of real property, groundwater, or surface water located at or adjacent to a facility where response actions are taken that are reasonably required to assure that the response actions are protective of public health or welfare or the environment. Institutional controls include restrictions, conditions, or controls enforceable by contract, easement, restrictive covenant, statute, ordinance, or rule, including official controls such as zoning, building codes, and official maps. An affidavit required under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115B.16#stat.115B.16.2">115B.16, subdivision 2</a>, or similar notice of a release recorded with real property records is also an institutional control.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115B.02.10">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 10.<span class="headnote">Natural resources.</span></h2>
          <p>"Natural resources" has the meaning given it in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116B.02#stat.116B.02.4">116B.02, subdivision 4</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115B.02.11">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 11.<span class="headnote">Owner of real property.</span></h2>
          <p>"Owner of real property" means a person who is in possession of, has the right of control, or controls the use of real property, including without limitation a person who may be a fee owner, lessee, renter, tenant, lessor, contract for deed vendee, licensor, licensee, or occupant; provided that:</p>
          <p>(1) a lessor of real property under a lease which in substance is a financing device and is treated as such under the United States Internal Revenue Code, common law, or statute, is not an owner of the real property;</p>
          <p>(2) a public utility holding a public utility easement is an owner of the real property described in the easement only for the purpose of carrying out the specific use for which the easement was granted;</p>
          <p>(3) any person holding a remainder or other nonpossessory interest or estate in real property is an owner of the real property beginning when that person's interest or estate in the real property vests in possession or that person obtains the unconditioned right to possession, or to control the use of, the real property; and</p>
          <p>(4) the state or an agency of the state is not an owner of real property solely because it holds title to the property in trust for taxing districts as a result of forfeiture of title for nonpayment of taxes.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115B.02.12">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 12.<span class="headnote">Person.</span></h2>
          <p>"Person" means any individual, partnership, association, public or private corporation or other entity including the United States government, any interstate body, the state and any agency, department or political subdivision of the state.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115B.02.13">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 13.<span class="headnote">Pollutant or contaminant.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) "Pollutant or contaminant" means any element, substance, compound, mixture, or agent, other than a hazardous substance, which after release from a facility and upon exposure of, ingestion, inhalation, or assimilation into any organism, either directly from the environment or indirectly by ingestion through food chains, will or may reasonably be anticipated to cause death, disease, behavioral abnormalities, cancer, genetic mutation, physiological malfunctions (including malfunctions in reproduction) or physical deformations, in the organisms or their offspring.</p>
          <p>(b) Pollutant or contaminant does not include natural gas, natural gas liquids, liquefied natural gas, synthetic gas usable for fuel, or mixtures of such synthetic gas and natural gas.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115B.02.14">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 14.<span class="headnote">Public utility easement.</span></h2>
          <p>"Public utility easement" means an easement used for the purposes of transmission, distribution, or furnishing, at wholesale or retail, natural or manufactured gas, or electric or telephone service, by a public utility as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/216B.02#stat.216B.02.4">216B.02, subdivision 4</a>, a cooperative electric association organized under the provisions of chapter 308A, a telephone company as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/237.01">237.01</a>, subdivisions 3 and 7, or a municipality producing or furnishing gas, electric, or telephone service.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115B.02.15">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 15.<span class="headnote">Release.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) "Release" means any spilling, leaking, pumping, pouring, emitting, emptying, discharging, injecting, escaping, leaching, dumping, or disposing into the environment which occurred at a point in time or which continues to occur.</p>
          <p>(b) Release does not include:</p>
          <p>(1) emissions from the engine exhaust of a motor vehicle, rolling stock, aircraft, watercraft, or pipeline pumping station engine;</p>
          <p>(2) release of source, by-product, or special nuclear material from a nuclear incident, as those terms are defined in the Atomic Energy Act of 1954, under United States Code, title 42, section 2014, if the release is subject to requirements with respect to financial protection established by the federal Nuclear Regulatory Commission under United States Code, title 42, section 2210;</p>
          <p>(3) release of source, by-product or special nuclear material from any processing site designated pursuant to the Uranium Mill Tailings Radiation Control Act of 1978, under United States Code, title 42, section 7912(a)(1) or 7942(a); or</p>
          <p>(4) any release resulting from the application of fertilizer or agricultural or silvicultural chemicals, or disposal of emptied pesticide containers or residues from a pesticide as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/18B.01#stat.18B.01.18">18B.01, subdivision 18</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115B.02.16">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 16.<span class="headnote">Remedy or remedial action.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) "Remedy" or "remedial action" means those actions consistent with permanent remedy taken instead of or in addition to removal actions in the event of a release or threatened release of a hazardous substance, or a pollutant or contaminant, into the environment, to prevent, minimize or eliminate the release in order to protect the public health or welfare or the environment.</p>
          <p>(b) Remedy or remedial action includes, but is not limited to:</p>
          <p>(1) actions at the location of the release such as storage, confinement, perimeter protection using dikes, trenches, or ditches, clay cover, neutralization, cleanup of released hazardous substances, pollutants or contaminants, or contaminated materials, recycling or reuse, diversion, destruction, segregation of reactive wastes, dredging or excavations, repair or replacement of leaking containers, collection of leachate and runoff, on-site treatment or incineration, provision of alternative water supplies, any monitoring and maintenance, and institutional controls reasonably required to assure that these actions protect the public health and welfare and the environment; and</p>
          <p>(2) the costs of permanent relocation of residents and businesses and community facilities when the agency determines that, alone or in combination with other measures, relocation is more cost-effective than and environmentally preferable to the transportation, storage, treatment, destruction, or secure disposition off-site of hazardous substances, or pollutants or contaminants, or may otherwise be necessary to protect the public health or welfare.</p>
          <p>(c) Remedy or remedial action does not include off-site transport of hazardous substances, pollutants or contaminants, or contaminated materials or their storage, treatment, destruction, or secure disposition off site unless the agency determines that these actions:</p>
          <p>(1) are more cost-effective than other remedial actions;</p>
          <p>(2) will create new capacity to manage hazardous substances in addition to those located at the affected facility, in compliance with section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.07">116.07</a> and subtitle C of the Solid Waste Disposal Act, United States Code, title 42, section 6921 et seq.; or</p>
          <p>(3) are necessary to protect the public health or welfare or the environment from a present or potential risk which may be created by further exposure to the continued presence of the hazardous substances, pollutants or contaminants, or contaminated materials.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115B.02.17">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 17.<span class="headnote">Remove or removal.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) "Remove" or "removal" means:</p>
          <p>(1) the cleanup or removal of a released hazardous substance, or a pollutant or contaminant, from the environment;</p>
          <p>(2) necessary actions taken in the event of a threatened release of a hazardous substance, or a pollutant or contaminant, into the environment;</p>
          <p>(3) actions necessary to monitor, test, analyze, and evaluate a release or threatened release of a hazardous substance, or a pollutant or contaminant;</p>
          <p>(4) disposal or processing of removed material; or</p>
          <p>(5) other actions necessary to prevent, minimize, or mitigate damage to the public health or welfare or the environment, which may otherwise result from a release or threatened release.</p>
          <p>(b) Remove or removal includes, but is not limited to, security fencing or other measures to limit access, provision of alternative water supplies, temporary evacuation and housing of threatened individuals not otherwise provided for, action taken pursuant to the federal Superfund Act, under United States Code, title 42, section 9604(b), and any emergency assistance which may be provided under the Disaster Relief Act of 1974, United States Code, title 42, section 5121 et seq.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115B.02.18">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 18.<span class="headnote">Respond or response.</span></h2>
          <p>"Respond" or "response" means remove, removal, remedy, and remedial action.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115B.02.19">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 19.<span class="headnote">Water.</span></h2>
          <p>"Water" has the meaning given to the term "waters of the state" in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115.01#stat.115.01.22">115.01, subdivision 22</a>.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.115B.02.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1983/0/121/#laws.0.2.0">1983 c 121 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/186/#laws.0.15.0">1987 c 186 s 15</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/209/#laws.2.1.0">1989 c 209 art 2 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/335/#laws.4.106.0">1989 c 335 art 4 s 106</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/356/#laws.0.7.0">1989 c 356 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/586/#laws.0.1.0">1990 c 586 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/597/#laws.0.52.0">1990 c 597 s 52</a>-54; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/216/#laws.0.99.0">1997 c 216 s 99</a>,100</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.115B.03">
        <h1 class="shn">115B.03 RESPONSIBLE PERSON.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115B.03.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">General rule.</span></h2>
          <p>For the purposes of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115B.01">115B.01</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115B.20">115B.20</a>, and except as provided in subdivisions 2 and 3, a person is responsible for a release or threatened release of a hazardous substance, or a pollutant or contaminant, from a facility if the person:</p>
          <p>(1) owned or operated the facility:</p>
          <p>(i) when the hazardous substance, or pollutant or contaminant, was placed or came to be located in or on the facility;</p>
          <p>(ii) when the hazardous substance, or pollutant or contaminant, was located in or on the facility but before the release; or</p>
          <p>(iii) during the time of the release or threatened release;</p>
          <p>(2) owned or possessed the hazardous substance, or pollutant or contaminant, and arranged, by contract, agreement or otherwise, for the disposal, treatment or transport for disposal or treatment of the hazardous substance, or pollutant or contaminant; or</p>
          <p>(3) knew or reasonably should have known that waste the person accepted for transport to a disposal or treatment facility contained a hazardous substance, or pollutant or contaminant, and either selected the facility to which it was transported or disposed of it in a manner contrary to law.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115B.03.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Employees and employers.</span></h2>
          <p>When a person who is responsible for a release or threatened release as provided in subdivision 1 is an employee who is acting in the scope of employment:</p>
          <p>(1) The employee is subject to liability under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115B.04">115B.04</a> or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115B.05">115B.05</a> only if the employee's conduct with respect to the hazardous substance was negligent under circumstances in which the employee knew that the substance was hazardous and that the conduct, if negligent, could result in serious harm.</p>
          <p>(2) The person's employer shall be considered a person responsible for the release or threatened release and is subject to liability under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115B.04">115B.04</a> or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115B.05">115B.05</a> regardless of the degree of care exercised by the employee.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115B.03.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Owner of real property.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) An owner of real property is not a person responsible for the release or threatened release of a hazardous substance from a facility in or on the property unless that person:</p>
          <p>(1) was engaged in the business of generating, transporting, storing, treating, or disposing of a hazardous substance at the facility or disposing of waste at the facility, or knowingly permitted others to engage in such a business at the facility;</p>
          <p>(2) knowingly permitted any person to make regular use of the facility for disposal of waste;</p>
          <p>(3) knowingly permitted any person to use the facility for disposal of a hazardous substance;</p>
          <p>(4) knew or reasonably should have known that a hazardous substance was located in or on the facility at the time right, title, or interest in the property was first acquired by the person and engaged in conduct associating that person with the release; or</p>
          <p>(5) took action which significantly contributed to the release after that person knew or reasonably should have known that a hazardous substance was located in or on the facility.</p>
          <p>(b) For the purpose of paragraph (a), clause (4), a written warranty, representation, or undertaking, which is set forth in an instrument conveying any right, title or interest in the real property and which is executed by the person conveying the right, title or interest, or which is set forth in any memorandum of any such instrument executed for the purpose of recording, is admissible as evidence of whether the person acquiring any right, title, or interest in the real property knew or reasonably should have known that a hazardous substance was located in or on the facility.</p>
          <p>(c) Any liability which accrues to an owner of real property under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115B.01">115B.01</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115B.15">115B.15</a> does not accrue to any other person who is not an owner of the real property merely because the other person holds some right, title, or interest in the real property.</p>
          <p>(d) An owner of real property on which a public utility easement is located is not a responsible person with respect to any release caused by any act or omission of the public utility which holds the easement in carrying out the specific use for which the easement was granted.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115B.03.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Tax-forfeited land.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The state, an agency of the state, or a political subdivision that may be considered an owner of tax-forfeited real property is not a person responsible for a release or threatened release from a facility in or on the property under subdivision 3, paragraph (a), clause (4).</p>
          <p>(b) The state, an agency of the state, or a political subdivision is not an operator of a facility in or on tax-forfeited land solely as a result of actions taken to manage, sell, or transfer the land in accordance with chapter 282 and other laws applicable to tax-forfeited land.</p>
          <p>(c) Nothing in this subdivision relieves the state, a state agency, or a political subdivision from liability for causing or significantly contributing to the release of a hazardous substance from a facility in or on the land.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115B.03.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Eminent domain.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The state, an agency of the state, or a political subdivision is not a responsible person under this section solely as a result of the acquisition of property, or as a result of providing funds for the acquisition of such property either through loan or grant, if the property was acquired by the state, an agency of the state, or a political subdivision:</p>
          <p>(1) through exercise of the power of eminent domain;</p>
          <p>(2) through negotiated purchase in lieu of, or after filing a petition for the taking of the property through eminent domain;</p>
          <p>(3) after adopting a redevelopment or development plan under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/469.001">469.001</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/469.133">469.133</a> describing the property and stating its intended use and the necessity of its taking;</p>
          <p>(4) after adopting a layout plan for highway development under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/161.15">161.15</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/161.241">161.241</a> describing the property and stating its intended use and the necessity of its taking; or</p>
          <p>(5) through the use of a loan to purchase right-of-way in the seven-county metropolitan area under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/473.167">473.167</a>.</p>
          <p>(b) A person who acquires property from the state, an agency of the state, or a political subdivision, is not a responsible person under this section solely as a result of the acquisition of property if the property was acquired by the state, agency, or political subdivision through exercise of the power of eminent domain or by negotiated purchase after filing a petition for the taking of the property through eminent domain, after adopting a redevelopment or development plan under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/469.001">469.001</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/469.133">469.133</a> describing the property and stating its intended use and the necessity of its taking, or after adopting a layout plan for highway development under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/161.15">161.15</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/161.241">161.241</a> describing the property and stating its intended use and the necessity of its taking.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115B.03.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Mortgages.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A mortgagee is not a responsible person under this section solely because the mortgagee becomes an owner of real property through foreclosure of the mortgage or by receipt of the deed to the mortgaged property in lieu of foreclosure.</p>
          <p>(b) A mortgagee of real property where a facility is located or a holder of a security interest in facility assets or inventory is not an operator of the facility for the purpose of this section solely because the mortgagee or holder has a capacity to influence the operation of the facility to protect its security interest in the real property or assets.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115B.03.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Contract for deed vendors.</span></h2>
          <p>A contract for deed vendor who is otherwise not a responsible party for a release or a threatened release of a hazardous substance from a facility is not a responsible person under this section solely as a result of a termination of the contract for deed under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/559.21">559.21</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115B.03.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Trustees.</span></h2>
          <p>A trustee who is not otherwise a responsible party for a release or threatened release of a hazardous substance from a facility is not a responsible person under this section solely because the facility is among the trust assets or solely because the trustee has the capacity to direct the operation of the facility.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115B.03.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">Personal representatives of estates.</span></h2>
          <p>A personal representative, guardian, or conservator of an estate who is not otherwise a responsible party for a release or threatened release of a hazardous substance from a facility is not a responsible person under this section solely because the facility is among the assets of the estate or solely because the personal representative, guardian, or conservator has the capacity to direct the operation of the facility.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115B.03.10">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 10.<span class="headnote">Contractor.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) For the purposes of this subdivision, "contractor" means a person who is not otherwise responsible for a release or threatened release of a hazardous substance, or a pollutant or contaminant, and who, under contract with another person:</p>
          <p>(1) performs response actions, including investigative, removal, or remedial actions to address the release or threatened release pursuant to a plan approved by the commissioner; or</p>
          <p>(2) performs development actions at the site of the release or threatened release, such as site preparation, engineering, construction, and similar actions with respect to which the commissioner approves a contingency plan or other conditions which the commissioner deems necessary to protect public health or welfare or the environment.</p>
          <p>(b) A contractor is not a responsible person for a release or threatened release solely as the result of performing response actions to address that release or threatened release if the contractor performs the response actions in accordance with a plan approved by the commissioner.</p>
          <p>(c) A contractor who performs development actions, such as site preparation, engineering, construction, or similar actions, at the site of a release or threatened release is not responsible for the release or threatened release solely as a result of performing the development actions if the contractor complies with a contingency plan or other conditions approved by the commissioner. The contractor must obtain approval from the commissioner for the contingency plan or other conditions:</p>
          <p>(1) for a site with a known release or threatened release, before the contractor commences the development actions; or</p>
          <p>(2) for a site with a release or threatened release discovered during the contractor's performance of the development actions, before the contractor performs further development actions at the site after discovery of the release or threatened release.</p>
          <p>(d) This subdivision shall not apply to a contractor who causes or contributes to a release or threatened release by an act or omission that is negligent, grossly negligent, or that constitutes intentional misconduct.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.115B.03.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1983/0/121/#laws.0.3.0">1983 c 121 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/586/#laws.0.2.0">1990 c 586 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/223/#laws.0.1.0">1991 c 223 s 1</a>-3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/347/#laws.2.1.0">1991 c 347 art 2 s 1</a>,2; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/168/#laws.0.1.0">1995 c 168 s 1</a>,2; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/359/#laws.0.1.0">1996 c 359 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/200/#laws.2.1.0">1997 c 200 art 2 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/341/#laws.0.1.0">1998 c 341 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/125/#laws.1.107.0">2013 c 125 art 1 s 107</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.115B.04">
        <h1 class="shn">115B.04 LIABILITY FOR RESPONSE COSTS AND NATURAL RESOURCES; LIMITATIONS AND DEFENSES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115B.04.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Liability.</span></h2>
          <p>Except as otherwise provided in subdivisions 2 to 12, and notwithstanding any other provision or rule of law, any person who is responsible for a release or threatened release of a hazardous substance from a facility is strictly liable, jointly and severally, for the following response costs and damages which result from the release or threatened release or to which the release or threatened release significantly contributes:</p>
          <p>(1) all reasonable and necessary response costs incurred by the state, a political subdivision of the state or the United States;</p>
          <p>(2) all reasonable and necessary removal costs incurred by any person; and</p>
          <p>(3) all damages for any injury to, destruction of, or loss of natural resources, including the reasonable costs of assessing such injury, destruction, or loss.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115B.04.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Liability for pollutant or contaminant excluded.</span></h2>
          <p>There is no liability under this section for response costs or damages which result from the release of a pollutant or contaminant.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115B.04.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Liability for threatened release.</span></h2>
          <p>Liability under this section for a threatened release of a hazardous substance is limited to the recovery by the agency of reasonable and necessary response costs as provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115B.17#stat.115B.17.6">115B.17, subdivision 6</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115B.04.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Liability of political subdivisions.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The liability of a political subdivision under this section is subject to the limits imposed under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/466.04#stat.466.04.1">466.04, subdivision 1</a>, except when the political subdivision is liable under this section as the owner or operator of a disposal facility as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.03#stat.115A.03.10">115A.03, subdivision 10</a>.</p>
          <p>(b) When a political subdivision is liable as an owner or operator of a disposal facility, the liability of each political subdivision is limited to $400,000 at each facility unless the facility was owned or operated under a valid joint powers agreement by three or more political subdivisions, in which case the aggregate liability of all political subdivisions that are parties to the joint powers agreement is limited to $1,200,000.</p>
          <p>(c) The limits on the liability of a political subdivision for ownership or operation of a disposal facility apply to the costs of response action incurred between the date a request for response action is issued by the agency and the date one year after the construction certificate of completion is approved by the commissioner, excluding the costs of negotiation of a consent order agreement.</p>
          <p>(d) When a political subdivision takes response action as the owner or operator of a disposal facility between the dates in paragraph (c), it may receive, after approval by the agency, reimbursement of any amount spent pursuant to an approved work plan that exceeds the applicable liability limit specified in this subdivision.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115B.04.4a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4a.<span class="headnote">Claims by mixed municipal solid waste disposal facilities.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Except as provided in paragraph (b), liability under this section for claims by owners or operators of mixed municipal solid waste disposal facilities that accept waste on or after April 9, 1994, and are not qualified facilities under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115B.39#stat.115B.39.2">115B.39, subdivision 2</a>, is limited to liability for response costs exceeding the amount of available financial assurance funds required under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.07#stat.116.07.4h">116.07, subdivision 4h</a>.</p>
          <p>(b) This subdivision does not affect liability under this section for claims based on the illegal disposal of waste at a facility.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115B.04.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Transporting household refuse.</span></h2>
          <p>A person who accepts only household refuse for transport to a treatment or disposal facility is not liable under this section for the release or threatened release of any hazardous substance unless that person knew or reasonably should have known that the hazardous substance was present in the refuse. For the purpose of this subdivision, household refuse means garbage, trash, or septic tank sanitary wastes generated by single or multiple residences, hotels, motels, restaurants and other similar facilities.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115B.04.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Defense to certain claims by political subdivisions and private persons.</span></h2>
          <p>It is a defense to a claim by a political subdivision or private person for recovery of the costs of its response actions under this section that the hazardous substance released from the facility was placed or came to be located in or on the facility before April 1, 1982, and that the response actions of the political subdivision or private person were not authorized by the agency as provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115B.17#stat.115B.17.12">115B.17, subdivision 12</a>. This defense applies only to response costs incurred on or after July 1, 1983.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115B.04.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Defense for intervening acts.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) It is a defense to liability under this section that the release or threatened release was caused solely by:</p>
          <p>(1) an act of God;</p>
          <p>(2) an act of war;</p>
          <p>(3) an act of vandalism or sabotage; or</p>
          <p>(4) an act or omission of a third party or the plaintiff.</p>
          <p>(b) "Third party" for the purposes of paragraph (a), clause (4), does not include an employee or agent of the defendant, or a person in the chain of responsibility for the generation, transportation, storage, treatment, or disposal of the hazardous substance.</p>
          <p>(c) The defenses provided in paragraph (a), clauses (3) and (4), apply only if the defendant establishes that the defendant exercised due care with respect to the hazardous substance concerned, taking into consideration the characteristics of the hazardous substance in light of all relevant facts and circumstances which the defendant knew or should have known, and that the defendant took precautions against foreseeable acts or omissions and the consequences that could foreseeably result from those acts or omissions.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115B.04.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Intervening acts of public agencies.</span></h2>
          <p>When the agency or the federal Environmental Protection Agency assumes control over any release or threatened release of a hazardous substance by taking removal actions at the site of the release, the persons responsible for the release are not liable under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115B.01">115B.01</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115B.15">115B.15</a> for any subsequent release of the hazardous substance from another facility to which it has been removed.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115B.04.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">Releases subject to certain permits or standards; federal postclosure fund.</span></h2>
          <p>It is a defense to liability under this section that:</p>
          <p>(1) the release or threatened release was from a hazardous waste facility as defined under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.03">115A.03</a>, for which a permit had been issued pursuant to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.07">116.07</a> or pursuant to subtitle C of the Solid Waste Disposal Act, United States Code, title 42, section 6921 et seq., the hazardous substance was specifically identified in the permit, and the release was within the limits allowed in the permit for release of that substance;</p>
          <p>(2) the hazardous substance released was specifically identified in a federal or state permit and the release is within the limits allowed in the permit;</p>
          <p>(3) the release resulted from circumstances identified and reviewed and made a part of the public record of a federal or state agency with respect to a permit issued or modified under federal or state law, and the release conformed with the permit;</p>
          <p>(4) the release was any part of an emission or discharge into the air or water and the emission or discharge was subject to a federal or state permit and was in compliance with control rules or regulations adopted pursuant to state or federal law;</p>
          <p>(5) the release was the introduction of any hazardous substance into a publicly owned treatment works and the substance was specified in, and is in compliance with, applicable pretreatment standards specified for that substance under state and federal law; or</p>
          <p>(6) liability has been assumed by the federal postclosure liability fund under United States Code, title 42, section 9607(k).</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115B.04.10">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 10.<span class="headnote">Natural resources.</span></h2>
          <p>It is a defense to liability under this section, for any injury to, destruction of, or loss of natural resources that:</p>
          <p>(1) the natural resources were specifically identified as an irreversible and irretrievable commitment of natural resources in an approved final state or federal environmental impact statement, or other comparable approved final environmental analysis for a project or facility which was the subject of a governmental permit or license; and</p>
          <p>(2) the project or facility was being operated within the terms of its permit or license.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115B.04.11">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 11.<span class="headnote">Rendering assistance in response actions.</span></h2>
          <p>It is a defense to liability under this section that the response costs or damages resulted from acts taken or omitted in preparation for, or in the course of rendering care, assistance, or advice to the commissioner or agency pursuant to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115B.17">115B.17</a> or in accordance with the national hazardous substance response plan pursuant to the federal Superfund Act, under United States Code, title 42, section 9605, or at the direction of an on-scene coordinator appointed under that plan, with respect to any release or threatened release of a hazardous substance.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115B.04.12">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 12.<span class="headnote">Burden of proof for defenses.</span></h2>
          <p>Any person claiming a defense provided in subdivisions 6 to 11 has the burden to prove all elements of the defense by a preponderance of the evidence.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.115B.04.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1983/0/121/#laws.0.4.0">1983 c 121 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/186/#laws.0.15.0">1987 c 186 s 15</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/325/#laws.0.29.0">1989 c 325 s 29</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/337/#laws.0.53.0">1991 c 337 s 53</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/639/#laws.1.1.0">1994 c 639 art 1 s 1</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.115B.05">
        <h1 class="shn">115B.05 LIABILITY FOR ECONOMIC LOSS, DEATH, PERSONAL INJURY, AND DISEASE; LIMITATIONS AND DEFENSES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115B.05.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Liability.</span></h2>
          <p>Except as otherwise provided in subdivisions 2 to 10, and notwithstanding any other provision or rule of law, any person who is responsible for the release of a hazardous substance from a facility is strictly liable for the following damages which result from the release or to which the release significantly contributes:</p>
          <p>(1) all damages for actual economic loss including:</p>
          <p>(i) any injury to, destruction of, or loss of any real or personal property, including relocation costs;</p>
          <p>(ii) any loss of use of real or personal property;</p>
          <p>(iii) any loss of past or future income or profits resulting from injury to, destruction of, or loss of real or personal property without regard to the ownership of the property; and</p>
          <p>(2) all damages for death, personal injury, or disease including:</p>
          <p>(i) any medical expenses, rehabilitation costs or burial expenses;</p>
          <p>(ii) any loss of past or future income, or loss of earning capacity; and</p>
          <p>(iii) damages for pain and suffering, including physical impairment.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115B.05.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Liability for pollutant or contaminant excluded.</span></h2>
          <p>There is no liability under this section for damages which result from the release of a pollutant or contaminant.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115B.05.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Certain employee claims not covered.</span></h2>
          <p>Except for a third party who is subject to liability under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/176.061#stat.176.061.5">176.061, subdivision 5</a>, there is no liability under this section for the death, personal injury or disease of an employee which is compensable under chapter 176 as an injury or disease arising out of and in the course of employment.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115B.05.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Liability limitations.</span></h2>
          <p>The liability of a political subdivision under this section is subject to the limits imposed under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/466.04#stat.466.04.1">466.04, subdivision 1</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115B.05.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Transporting household refuse.</span></h2>
          <p>A person who accepts only household refuse for transport to a treatment or disposal facility is not liable under this section for the release or threatened release of any hazardous substance unless that person knew or reasonably should have known that the hazardous substance was present in the refuse. For the purpose of this subdivision, household refuse means garbage, trash, or septic tank sanitary wastes generated by single or multiple residences, hotels, motels, restaurants and other similar facilities.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115B.05.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Defense for intervening acts.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) It is a defense to liability under this section that the release or threatened release was caused solely by:</p>
          <p>(1) an act of God;</p>
          <p>(2) an act of war;</p>
          <p>(3) an act of vandalism or sabotage; or</p>
          <p>(4) an act or omission of a third party or the plaintiff.</p>
          <p>(b) "Third party" for the purposes of paragraph (a), clause (4), does not include an employee or agent of the defendant, or a person in the chain of responsibility for the generation, transportation, storage, treatment, or disposal of the hazardous substance.</p>
          <p>(c) The defenses provided in paragraph (a), clauses (3) and (4), apply only if the defendant establishes that the defendant exercised due care with respect to the hazardous substance concerned, taking into consideration the characteristics of the hazardous substance in light of all relevant facts and circumstances which the defendant knew or should have known, and that the defendant took precautions against foreseeable acts or omissions and the consequences that could foreseeably result from those acts or omissions.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115B.05.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Intervening acts of public agencies.</span></h2>
          <p>When the agency or the federal Environmental Protection Agency assumes control over any release or threatened release of a hazardous substance by taking removal actions at the site of the release, the persons responsible for the release are not liable under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115B.01">115B.01</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115B.15">115B.15</a> for any subsequent release of the hazardous substance from another facility to which it has been removed.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115B.05.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Releases subject to certain permits or standards; federal postclosure fund.</span></h2>
          <p>It is a defense to liability under this section that:</p>
          <p>(1) the release or threatened release was from a hazardous waste facility as defined under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.03">115A.03</a>, for which a permit had been issued pursuant to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.07">116.07</a> or pursuant to subtitle C of the Solid Waste Disposal Act, United States Code, title 42, section 6921 et seq., the hazardous substance was specifically identified in the permit, and the release was within the limits allowed in the permit for release of that substance;</p>
          <p>(2) the hazardous substance released was specifically identified in a federal or state permit and the release is within the limits allowed in the permit;</p>
          <p>(3) the release resulted from circumstances identified and reviewed and made a part of the public record of a federal or state agency with respect to a permit issued or modified under federal or state law, and the release conformed with the permit;</p>
          <p>(4) the release was any part of an emission or discharge into the air or water and the emission or discharge was subject to a federal or state permit and was in compliance with control rules or regulations adopted pursuant to state or federal law;</p>
          <p>(5) the release was the introduction of any hazardous substance into a publicly owned treatment works and the substance was specified in, and is in compliance with, applicable pretreatment standards specified for that substance under state and federal law; or</p>
          <p>(6) liability has been assumed by the federal postclosure liability fund under United States Code, title 42, section 9607(k).</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115B.05.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">Rendering assistance in response actions.</span></h2>
          <p>It is a defense to liability under this section that the damages resulted from acts taken or omitted in preparation for, or in the course of rendering care, assistance, or advice to the commissioner or agency pursuant to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115B.17">115B.17</a> or in accordance with the national hazardous substance response plan pursuant to the federal Superfund Act, under United States Code, title 42, section 9605, or at the direction of an on-scene coordinator appointed under that plan, with respect to any release or threatened release of a hazardous substance.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115B.05.10">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 10.<span class="headnote">Burden of proof for defenses.</span></h2>
          <p>Any person claiming a defense provided in subdivisions 6 to 9 has the burden to prove all elements of the defense by a preponderance of the evidence.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.115B.05.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1983/0/121/#laws.0.5.0">1983 c 121 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/1/8/#laws.0.1.0">1Sp1985 c 8 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/186/#laws.0.15.0">1987 c 186 s 15</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.115B.055">
        <h1 class="shn">115B.055 EFFECT OF REMOVING AND REPEALING CERTAIN PROVISIONS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115B.055.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Joint and several liability for personal injury.</span></h2>
          <p>The enactment of Laws 1983, chapter 121, section 5, relating to joint and several liability, and the subsequent amendment of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115B.05">115B.05</a> as provided in Laws 1985, First Special Session chapter 8, shall not be construed in any way as a determination of legislative intent regarding the applicability of joint and several liability in any action brought under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115B.05">115B.05</a>. The determination of whether joint and several liability applies in any action brought under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115B.05">115B.05</a> shall be based solely on applicable statutory and common law.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115B.055.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Causation of personal injury.</span></h2>
          <p>In any action brought under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115B.05">115B.05</a>, or under any other law, to recover damages for death, personal injury, or disease arising out of the release of a hazardous substance, the enactment of Laws 1983, chapter 121, section 7, and subsequent repeal of Minnesota Statutes 1984, section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115B.07">115B.07</a>, under Laws 1985, First Special Session chapter 8, relating to proof of causation, shall not be construed in any way as a determination of legislative intent regarding the legal principles applicable to the proof of the causal connection between the release and the death, injury, or disease. The legal principles applicable to the proof of causation shall be determined solely on the basis of applicable statutory and common law.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.115B.055.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/1/8/#laws.0.17.0">1Sp1985 c 8 s 17</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.115B.06">
        <h1 class="shn">115B.06 APPLICATION TO PAST ACTIONS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115B.06.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Application of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115B.05">115B.05</a>. </span></h2>
          <p>Section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115B.05">115B.05</a> does not apply to any claim for damages arising out of the release of a hazardous substance which was placed or came to be located in or on the facility wholly before July 1, 1983.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115B.06.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/1/8/#laws.0.19.0">1Sp1985 c 8 s 19</a>]</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.115B.06.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1983/0/121/#laws.0.6.0">1983 c 121 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/1/8/#laws.0.2.0">1Sp1985 c 8 s 2</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.115B.07"><b>115B.07</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/1/8/#laws.0.19.0">1Sp1985 c 8 s 19</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.115B.08">
        <h1 class="shn">115B.08 APPORTIONMENT AND CONTRIBUTION.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115B.08.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Right of apportionment; factors.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Any person held jointly and severally liable under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115B.04">115B.04</a> has the right at trial to have the trier of fact apportion liability among the parties as provided in this section. The burden is on each defendant to show how that defendant's liability should be apportioned. The court shall reduce the amount of damages in proportion to any amount of liability apportioned to the party recovering.</p>
          <p>(b) In apportioning the liability of any party under this section, the trier of fact shall consider the following:</p>
          <p>(1) the extent to which that party's contribution to the release of a hazardous substance can be distinguished;</p>
          <p>(2) the amount of hazardous substance involved;</p>
          <p>(3) the degree of toxicity of the hazardous substance involved;</p>
          <p>(4) the degree of involvement of and care exercised by the party in manufacturing, treating, transporting, and disposing of the hazardous substance;</p>
          <p>(5) the degree of cooperation by the party with federal, state, or local officials to prevent any harm to the public health or the environment; and</p>
          <p>(6) knowledge by the party of the hazardous nature of the substance.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115B.08.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Contribution.</span></h2>
          <p>If a person is held jointly and severally liable under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115B.04">115B.04</a> and establishes a proportionate share of the aggregate liability, the provisions of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/604.02">604.02</a>, subdivisions 1 and 2, shall apply with respect to contribution and reallocation of any uncollectible amounts.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.115B.08.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1983/0/121/#laws.0.8.0">1983 c 121 s 8</a>; 1986 c 444</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.115B.09">
        <h1 class="shn">115B.09 COMPARATIVE FAULT AND CONTRIBUTION.</h1>
        <p>The provisions of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/604.01">604.01</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/604.02">604.02</a>, subdivisions 1 and 2, apply to any action for damages under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115B.05">115B.05</a>.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.115B.09.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1983/0/121/#laws.0.9.0">1983 c 121 s 9</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/1/8/#laws.0.3.0">1Sp1985 c 8 s 3</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.115B.10">
        <h1 class="shn">115B.10 NO AVOIDANCE OF LIABILITY; INSURANCE AND SUBROGATION.</h1>
        <p>An owner or operator of a facility or any other person who may be liable under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115B.01">115B.01</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115B.15">115B.15</a> may not avoid that liability by means of any conveyance of any right, title, or interest in real property, or by any indemnification, hold harmless agreement, or similar agreement. Nothing in this section shall be construed:</p>
        <p>(1) to prohibit any party who may be liable under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115B.01">115B.01</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115B.15">115B.15</a> from entering an agreement by which that party is insured, held harmless or indemnified for part or all of that liability;</p>
        <p>(2) to prohibit the enforcement of any insurance, hold harmless or indemnification agreement; or</p>
        <p>(3) to bar any cause of action brought by a party who may be liable under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115B.01">115B.01</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115B.15">115B.15</a> or by an insurer or guarantor, whether by right of subrogation or otherwise.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.115B.10.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1983/0/121/#laws.0.10.0">1983 c 121 s 10</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.115B.11">
        <h1 class="shn">115B.11 STATUTE OF LIMITATIONS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115B.11.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Construction.</span></h2>
          <p>For the purposes of this section, "construction" means actions taken after the selection of remedial action such as excavation, building of structures, installation of equipment or fixtures, and other physical actions to respond to a release or threatened release.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115B.11.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Action to recover costs.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) An action for recovery of response costs under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115B.04">115B.04</a>, including recovery of costs and expenses under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115B.17#stat.115B.17.6">115B.17, subdivision 6</a>, may be commenced any time after costs and expenses have been incurred but must be commenced no later than six years after initiation of physical on-site construction of a response action.</p>
          <p>(b) A party prevailing in an action commenced within the time required under paragraph (a) shall be entitled to a declaratory judgment of liability for all future reasonable and necessary costs incurred by that party to respond to the release or threatened release, including costs and expenses under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115B.17#stat.115B.17.6">115B.17, subdivision 6</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115B.11.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Action for damages.</span></h2>
          <p>No person may recover damages pursuant to sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115B.01">115B.01</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115B.15">115B.15</a> unless the action is commenced within six years from the date when the cause of action accrues. In determining when the cause of action accrues for an action to recover damages for death, personal injury or disease, the court shall consider factors including the following:</p>
          <p>(1) when the plaintiff discovered the injury or loss;</p>
          <p>(2) whether a personal injury or disease had sufficiently manifested itself; and</p>
          <p>(3) when the plaintiff discovered, or using due diligence should have discovered, a causal connection between the injury, disease, or loss and the release of a hazardous substance.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115B.11.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Application.</span></h2>
          <p>Subdivisions 1 to 3 apply to actions for recovery of costs commenced on or after April 1, 1998. Response costs incurred before April 1, 1998, are recoverable in an action commenced on or after April 1, 1998, only if physical on-site construction of the response action was initiated not more than six years before the cost recovery action is commenced. Notwithstanding any provision in Laws 1998, chapter 341, to the contrary, the running of the statute of limitations imposed by subdivisions 1 to 3 with respect to cost recovery actions is suspended until July 1, 1999. Subdivisions 1 to 3 shall not apply to any litigation pending in court on March 31, 1998, if the statute of limitations under this chapter has been contested in the litigation. Subdivisions 1 to 3 shall not be offered by any party as evidence of the intent, meaning, or application of the statute of limitations under this chapter.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.115B.11.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1983/0/121/#laws.0.11.0">1983 c 121 s 11</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/341/#laws.0.2.0">1998 c 341 s 2</a>,5</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.115B.12">
        <h1 class="shn">115B.12 OTHER REMEDIES PRESERVED.</h1>
        <p>Nothing in sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115B.01">115B.01</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115B.15">115B.15</a> shall be construed to abolish or diminish any remedy or affect the right of any person to bring a legal action or use any remedy available under any other provision of state or federal law, including common law, to recover for personal injury, disease, economic loss or response costs arising out of a release of any hazardous substance, or for removal or the costs of removal of that hazardous substance. Nothing in sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115B.01">115B.01</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115B.15">115B.15</a> shall be construed to limit or restrict in any way the liability of any person under any other state or federal law, including common law, for loss due to personal injury or disease, for economic loss, or for response costs arising out of any release or threatened release of a hazardous substance from a facility regardless of the time at which a hazardous substance was placed or came to be located in or on the facility. The provisions of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115B.01">115B.01</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115B.15">115B.15</a> shall not be considered, interpreted, or construed in any way as reflecting a determination, in whole or in part, of policy regarding the inapplicability of strict liability, or strict liability doctrines under any other state or federal law, including common law, to activities past, present or future, relating to hazardous substances, or pollutants or contaminants, or other similar activities.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.115B.12.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1983/0/121/#laws.0.12.0">1983 c 121 s 12</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.115B.13">
        <h1 class="shn">115B.13 DOUBLE RECOVERY PROHIBITED.</h1>
        <p>A person who recovers response costs or damages pursuant to sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115B.01">115B.01</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115B.15">115B.15</a> may not recover the same costs or damages pursuant to any other law. A person who recovers response costs or damages pursuant to any other state or federal law may not recover for the same costs or damages pursuant to sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115B.01">115B.01</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115B.15">115B.15</a>.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.115B.13.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1983/0/121/#laws.0.13.0">1983 c 121 s 13</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.115B.14">
        <h1 class="shn">115B.14 AWARDING COSTS.</h1>
        <p>Upon motion of a party prevailing in an action under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115B.01">115B.01</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115B.15">115B.15</a> the court may award costs, disbursements and reasonable attorney fees and witness fees to that party.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.115B.14.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1983/0/121/#laws.0.14.0">1983 c 121 s 14</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.115B.15">
        <h1 class="shn">115B.15 APPLICABILITY.</h1>
        <p>Sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115B.01">115B.01</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115B.14">115B.14</a> apply to any release or threatened release of a hazardous substance occurring on or after July 1, 1983, including any release which began before July 1, 1983, and continued after that date. Sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115B.01">115B.01</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115B.14">115B.14</a> do not apply to a release or threatened release which occurred wholly before July 1, 1983, regardless of the date of discovery of any injury or loss caused by the release or threatened release.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.115B.15.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1983/0/121/#laws.0.15.0">1983 c 121 s 15</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.115B.16">
        <h1 class="shn">115B.16 DISPOSITION OF FACILITIES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115B.16.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Closed disposal facilities; using property.</span></h2>
          <p>No person shall use any property on or in which hazardous waste remains after closure of a disposal facility as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.03#stat.115A.03.10">115A.03, subdivision 10</a>, in any way that disturbs the integrity of the final cover, liners, or any other components of any containment system, or the function of the disposal facility's monitoring systems, unless the agency finds that the disturbance:</p>
          <p>(1) is necessary to the proposed use of the property, and will not increase the potential hazard to human health or the environment; or</p>
          <p>(2) is necessary to reduce a threat to human health or the environment.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115B.16.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Recording affidavit.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Before any transfer of ownership of any property which the owner knew or should have known was used as the site of a hazardous waste disposal facility as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.03#stat.115A.03.10">115A.03, subdivision 10</a>, or which the owner knew or should have known is subject to extensive contamination by release of a hazardous substance, the owner shall record with the county recorder of the county in which the property is located an affidavit containing a legal description of the property that discloses to any potential transferee:</p>
          <p>(1) that the land has been used to dispose of hazardous waste or that the land is contaminated by a release of a hazardous substance;</p>
          <p>(2) the identity, quantity, location, condition and circumstances of the disposal or contamination to the full extent known or reasonably ascertainable; and</p>
          <p>(3) that the use of the property or some portion of it may be restricted as provided in subdivision 1.</p>
          <p>(b) An owner must also file an affidavit within 60 days after any material change in any matter required to be disclosed under paragraph (a), clauses (1) to (3), with respect to property for which an affidavit has already been recorded.</p>
          <p>(c) If the owner or any subsequent owner of the property removes the hazardous substance, together with any residues, liner, and contaminated underlying and surrounding soil, that owner may record an affidavit indicating the removal of the hazardous substance.</p>
          <p>(d) Failure to record an affidavit as provided in this subdivision does not affect or prevent any transfer of ownership of the property.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115B.16.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Duty of county recorder.</span></h2>
          <p>The county recorder shall record all affidavits presented in accordance with subdivision 2. The affidavits shall be recorded in a manner which will assure their disclosure in the ordinary course of a title search of the subject property.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115B.16.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Penalties.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Any person who knowingly violates the provisions of subdivision 1 is subject to a civil penalty in an amount determined by the court of not more than $100,000, and shall be liable under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115B.04">115B.04</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115B.05">115B.05</a> for any release or threatened release of any hazardous substance resulting from the violation.</p>
          <p>(b) Any person who knowingly fails to record an affidavit as required by subdivision 2 shall be liable under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115B.04">115B.04</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115B.05">115B.05</a> for any release or threatened release of any hazardous substance from a facility located on that property.</p>
          <p>(c) A civil penalty may be imposed and recovered by an action brought by a county attorney or by the attorney general in the district court of the county in which the property is located.</p>
          <p>(d) Any civil fines recovered under this subdivision shall be deposited in the remediation fund.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.115B.16.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1983/0/121/#laws.0.16.0">1983 c 121 s 16</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/335/#laws.4.106.0">1989 c 335 art 4 s 106</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2004/0/228/#laws.1.24.0">2004 c 228 art 1 s 24</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.115B.17">
        <h1 class="shn">115B.17 STATE RESPONSE TO RELEASES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115B.17.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Removal and remedial action.</span></h2>
          <p>Whenever there is a release or substantial threat of release from a facility of any pollutant or contaminant which presents an imminent and substantial danger to the public health or welfare or the environment or whenever a hazardous substance is released or there is a threatened release of a hazardous substance from a facility:</p>
          <p>(a) The agency may take any removal or remedial action relating to the hazardous substance, or pollutant or contaminant, which the agency deems necessary to protect the public health or welfare or the environment. Before taking any action the agency shall:</p>
          <p>(1) Request any responsible party known to the agency to take actions which the agency deems reasonable and necessary to protect the public health or welfare or the environment, stating the reasons for the actions, a reasonable time for beginning and completing the actions taking into account the urgency of the actions for protecting the public health or welfare or the environment, and the intention of the agency to take action if the requested actions are not taken as requested;</p>
          <p>(2) Notify the owner of real property where the facility is located or where response actions are proposed to be taken, if the owner is not a responsible party, that responsible parties have been requested to take response actions and that the owner's cooperation will be required in order for responsible parties or the agency to take those actions; and</p>
