        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/1/8/#laws.0.4.0">1Sp1985 c 8 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/325/#laws.0.32.0">1989 c 325 s 32</a>-39; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/335/#laws.4.42.0">1989 c 335 art 4 s 42</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/199/#laws.1.19.0">1991 c 199 art 1 s 19</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/220/#laws.0.99.0">1995 c 220 s 99</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/254/#laws.1.27.0">1998 c 254 art 1 s 27</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2002/0/379/#laws.2.3.0">2002 c 379 art 2 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/128/#laws.2.14.0">2003 c 128 art 2 s 14</a>,15; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2011/0/76/#laws.1.8.0">2011 c 76 art 1 s 8</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.115B.26">
        <h1 class="shn">115B.26 PAYING CLAIMS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115B.26.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/220/#laws.0.141.0">1995 c 220 s 141</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115B.26.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Appropriation.</span></h2>
          <p>The amount necessary to pay claims of compensation granted by the agency under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115B.25">115B.25</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115B.37">115B.37</a> must be directly appropriated to the agency from the fund by the legislature. The agency shall submit claims for compensation to the legislature at the next legislative session.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115B.26.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed by amendment, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/128/#laws.2.16.0">2003 c 128 art 2 s 16</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115B.26.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Transfer request.</span></h2>
          <p>At the end of each fiscal year, the agency shall submit a request to the Petroleum Tank Release Compensation Board for transfer to the fund from the petroleum tank release cleanup fund under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115C.08#stat.115C.08.5">115C.08, subdivision 5</a>, of an amount equal to the compensation granted by the agency for claims related to petroleum releases plus administrative costs related to determination of those claims.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.115B.26.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/1/8/#laws.0.5.0">1Sp1985 c 8 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/325/#laws.0.40.0">1989 c 325 s 40</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/335/#laws.4.43.0">1989 c 335 art 4 s 43</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/199/#laws.1.20.0">1991 c 199 art 1 s 20</a>,21; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/220/#laws.0.100.0">1995 c 220 s 100</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/254/#laws.1.96.0">1995 c 254 art 1 s 96</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2002/0/379/#laws.2.4.0">2002 c 379 art 2 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/128/#laws.2.16.0">2003 c 128 art 2 s 16</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.115B.27"><b>115B.27</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2002/0/379/#laws.2.24.0">2002 c 379 art 2 s 24</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.115B.28">
        <h1 class="shn">115B.28 POWERS AND DUTIES OF AGENCY.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115B.28.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Duties.</span></h2>
          <p>In addition to performing duties specified in sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115B.25">115B.25</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115B.37">115B.37</a> or in other law, and subject to the limitations on disclosure contained in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115B.35">115B.35</a>, the agency shall:</p>
          <p>(1) adopt rules, including rules governing practice and procedure before the agency, the form and procedure for applications for compensation, and procedures for claims investigations;</p>
          <p>(2) publicize the availability of compensation and application procedures on a statewide basis with special emphasis on geographical areas surrounding sites identified by the agency as having releases from a facility where a harmful substance was placed or came to be located prior to July 1, 1983;</p>
          <p>(3) collect, analyze, and make available to the public, in consultation with the Department of Health, the Pollution Control Agency, the University of Minnesota Medical and Public Health Schools, and the medical community, data regarding injuries relating to exposure to harmful substances; and</p>
          <p>(4) prepare and transmit the legislative report required under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115B.20">115B.20, subdivision </a>6, to include (i) a summary of agency activity under clause (3); (ii) data determined by the agency from actual cases, including but not limited to number of cases, actual compensation received by each claimant, types of cases, and types of injuries compensated, as they relate to types of harmful substances as well as length of exposure, but excluding identification of the claimants; (iii) all administrative costs associated with the business of the agency; and (iv) agency recommendations for legislative changes, further study, or any other recommendation aimed at improving the system of compensation.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115B.28.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Powers.</span></h2>
          <p>In addition to exercising any powers specified in sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115B.25">115B.25</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115B.37">115B.37</a> or in other law, the agency may:</p>
          <p>(1) in reviewing a claim, consider any information relevant to the claim, in accordance with the evidentiary standards contained in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115B.35">115B.35</a>;</p>
          <p>(2) contract for consultant or other services necessary to carry out the agency's duties under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115B.25">115B.25</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115B.37">115B.37</a>;</p>
          <p>(3) grant reasonable partial compensation on an emergency basis pending the final decision on a claim if the claim is one with respect to which an award will probably be made and undue hardship will result to the claimant if immediate payment is not made;</p>
          <p>(4) limit access to information collected and maintained by the agency and take any other action necessary to protect not public data as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/13.02#stat.13.02.8a">13.02, subdivision 8a</a>, and protected information, in accordance with the limitations contained in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115B.35">115B.35</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115B.28.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Investigation; obtaining information.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The agency may investigate any claim for compensation and for this purpose it may require from the claimant and request from any person information regarding any matter, fact, or circumstance which is relevant to determination of a claim under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115B.33">115B.33</a>. In exercising its powers under this subdivision, the agency may collect information reasonably calculated to lead to the discovery of evidence admissible under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115B.35">115B.35</a>. The agency shall reimburse the person requested to provide information the actual cost of copies of documents, papers, samples, or other tangible items necessary to respond to the request from the agency. In order to obtain this information the agency, subject to any applicable privilege, may:</p>
          <p>(1) request any person to produce documents, papers, books, or other tangible things in the possession, custody, or control of that person;</p>
          <p>(2) request the sworn testimony of any person as to any relevant fact or opinion;</p>
          <p>(3) direct written questions to any person and request written answers and objections;</p>
          <p>(4) request a mental or physical examination of the claimant or autopsy of any deceased person whose death is the basis of the claim, provided that notice is given to the claimant and the claimant receives a copy of the report; and</p>
          <p>(5) request a waiver of medical privilege by the claimant.</p>
          <p>(b) The agency shall give written notice of any request under this subdivision at least 15 days before the person is expected to comply with the request. If a person fails or refuses to comply with a request for information relevant to the release of a harmful substance, the agency may issue a subpoena for the production of the information and may petition the district court for an order enforcing the subpoena. If a person fails or refuses to comply with a request for other information relevant to determination of the claim, the agency may petition the district court for an order to compel compliance with the request. If the claimant refuses to comply with a request by the agency for information relevant to the claim, the agency may dismiss the claim.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115B.28.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Information from state agencies.</span></h2>
          <p>In order to perform its duties, the agency may request information from the supervising officer of any state agency or state institution of higher education. When requesting health data as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/13.3805#stat.13.3805.1">13.3805, subdivision 1</a>, or sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/144.671">144.671</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/144.69">144.69</a>, the agency must submit a written release signed by the subject of the data or, if the subject is deceased, a representative of the deceased, authorizing release of the data in whole or in part. The supervising officer shall comply with the agency's request to the extent possible considering available agency or institution appropriations and may assign agency or institution employees to assist the agency in performing its duties under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115B.25">115B.25</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115B.37">115B.37</a>.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.115B.28.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/1/8/#laws.0.7.0">1Sp1985 c 8 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/209/#laws.0.1.0">1987 c 209 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/325/#laws.0.42.0">1989 c 325 s 42</a>,78; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/199/#laws.2.1.0">1991 c 199 art 2 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/227/#laws.0.22.0">1999 c 227 s 22</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2002/0/379/#laws.2.5.0">2002 c 379 art 2 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/114/#laws.4.81.0">2013 c 114 art 4 s 81</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.115B.29">
        <h1 class="shn">115B.29 ELIGIBLE PERSONS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115B.29.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Personal injury and certain property claims.</span></h2>
          <p>A person may file a claim with the agency pursuant to this section for compensation for an eligible injury, or for eligible property damage that could reasonably have resulted from an exposure in Minnesota to a harmful substance released from a facility.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115B.29.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/325/#laws.0.77.0">1989 c 325 s 77</a>]</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.115B.29.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/1/8/#laws.0.8.0">1Sp1985 c 8 s 8</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/325/#laws.0.43.0">1989 c 325 s 43</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2002/0/379/#laws.2.6.0">2002 c 379 art 2 s 6</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.115B.30">
        <h1 class="shn">115B.30 ELIGIBLE INJURY AND DAMAGE.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115B.30.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Eligible personal injury.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A personal injury which could reasonably have resulted from exposure to a harmful substance released from a facility where it was placed or came to be located is eligible for compensation from the fund if:</p>
          <p>(1) it is a medically verified chronic or progressive disease, illness, or disability such as cancer, organic nervous system disorders, or physical deformities, including malfunctions in reproduction, in humans or their offspring, or death; or</p>
          <p>(2) it is a medically verified acute disease or condition that typically manifests itself rapidly after a single exposure or limited exposures and the persons responsible for the release of the harmful substance are unknown or cannot with reasonable diligence be determined or located or a judgment would not be satisfied in whole or in part against the persons determined to be responsible for the release of the harmful substance.</p>
          <p>(b) A personal injury is not compensable from the account if:</p>
          <p>(1) the injury is compensable under the workers' compensation law, chapter 176;</p>
          <p>(2) the injury arises out of the claimant's use of a consumer product;</p>
          <p>(3) the injury arises out of an exposure that occurred or is occurring outside the geographical boundaries of the state;</p>
          <p>(4) the injury results from the release of a harmful substance for which the claimant is a responsible person; or</p>
          <p>(5) the injury is an acute disease or condition other than one described in paragraph (a).</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115B.30.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Eligible property damage.</span></h2>
          <p>Damage to real property in Minnesota owned by the claimant is eligible for compensation from the fund if the damage results from the presence in or on the property of a harmful substance released from a facility where it was placed or came to be located. Damage to property is not eligible for compensation from the fund if it results from the release of a harmful substance for which the claimant is a responsible person.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115B.30.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Time for filing claim.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A claim is not eligible for compensation from the fund unless it is filed with the agency within the time provided in this subdivision.</p>
          <p>(b) A claim for compensation for personal injury must be filed within two years after the injury and its connection to exposure to a harmful substance was or reasonably should have been discovered.</p>
          <p>(c) A claim for compensation for property damage must be filed within two years after the full amount of compensable losses can be determined.</p>
          <p>(d) Notwithstanding the provisions of this subdivision, claims for compensation that would otherwise be barred by any statute of limitations provided in sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115B.25">115B.25</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115B.37">115B.37</a> may be filed not later than January 1, 1992.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.115B.30.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/1/8/#laws.0.9.0">1Sp1985 c 8 s 9</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/325/#laws.0.44.0">1989 c 325 s 44</a>,78; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/335/#laws.4.106.0">1989 c 335 art 4 s 106</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/199/#laws.1.22.0">1991 c 199 art 1 s 22</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2002/0/379/#laws.2.7.0">2002 c 379 art 2 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/128/#laws.2.17.0">2003 c 128 art 2 s 17</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.115B.31">
        <h1 class="shn">115B.31 OTHER ACTIONS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115B.31.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Subsequent action or claim prohibited in certain cases.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A person who has settled a claim for an eligible injury or eligible property damage with a responsible person, either before or after bringing an action in court for that injury or damage, may not file a claim with the fund for the same injury or damage. A person who has received a favorable judgment in a court action for an eligible injury or eligible property damage may not file a claim with the fund for the same injury or damage, unless the judgment cannot be satisfied in whole or in part against the persons responsible for the release of the harmful substance. A person who has filed a claim with the agency or its predecessor, the Harmful Substance Compensation Board, may not file another claim with the agency for the same eligible injury or damage, unless the claim was inactivated by the agency or board as provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115B.32#stat.115B.32.1">115B.32, subdivision 1</a>.</p>
          <p>(b) A person who has filed a claim with the agency or board for an eligible injury or damage, and who has received and accepted an award from the agency or board, is precluded from bringing an action in court for the same eligible injury or damage.</p>
          <p>(c) A person who files a claim with the agency for personal injury or property damage must include all known claims eligible for compensation in one proceeding before the agency.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115B.31.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Use of protected information and agency findings.</span></h2>
          <p>The findings and decision of the agency are inadmissible in any court action. Protected information may not be used in any court action except to the extent that the information is otherwise available to a party or discovered under the applicable Rules of Civil or Criminal Procedure.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115B.31.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Subrogation by state.</span></h2>
          <p>The state is subrogated to all the claimant's rights under statutory or common law to recover losses compensated from the fund from other sources, including responsible persons as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115B.03">115B.03</a>. The state may bring a subrogation action in its own name or in the name of the claimant. The state may not bring a subrogation action against a person who was a party in a court action by the claimant for the same eligible injury or damage, unless the claimant dismissed the action prior to trial. Money recovered by the state under this subdivision must be deposited in the fund. Nothing in sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115B.25">115B.25</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115B.37">115B.37</a> shall be construed to create a standard of recovery in a subrogation action.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115B.31.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Simultaneous claim and court action prohibited.</span></h2>
          <p>A claimant may not commence a court action to recover for any injury or damage for which the claimant seeks compensation from the fund during the time that a claim is pending before the agency. A person may not file a claim with the agency for compensation for any injury or damage for which the claimant seeks to recover in a pending court action. The time for filing a claim under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115B.30">115B.30</a> or the statute of limitations for any civil action is suspended during the period of time that a claimant is precluded from filing a claim or commencing an action under this subdivision.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.115B.31.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/1/8/#laws.0.10.0">1Sp1985 c 8 s 10</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/325/#laws.0.78.0">1989 c 325 s 78</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/199/#laws.1.23.0">1991 c 199 art 1 s 23</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2002/0/379/#laws.2.8.0">2002 c 379 art 2 s 8</a>-10; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/128/#laws.2.18.0">2003 c 128 art 2 s 18</a>-20; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2004/0/228/#laws.1.26.0">2004 c 228 art 1 s 26</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.115B.32">
        <h1 class="shn">115B.32 CLAIM FOR COMPENSATION.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115B.32.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Form.</span></h2>
          <p>A claim for compensation from the fund must be filed with the agency in the form required by the agency. When a claim does not include all the information required by subdivision 2 and applicable agency rules, the agency staff shall notify the claimant of the absence of the required information within 14 days of the filing of the claim. All required information must be received by the agency not later than 60 days after the claimant received notice of its absence or the claim will be inactivated and may not be resubmitted for at least one year following the date of inactivation. The agency may decide not to inactivate a claim under this subdivision if it finds serious extenuating circumstances.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115B.32.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Required information.</span></h2>
          <p>A claimant must provide the following information as part of the claim, provided that nothing in this chapter shall be construed to require the claimant to initiate a court action before filing a claim:</p>
          <p>(1) a sworn verification by the claimant of the facts set forth in the claim to the best of the claimant's knowledge;</p>
          <p>(2) evidence that the claimant is an eligible person;</p>
          <p>(3) evidence of the claimant's exposure to a named harmful substance;</p>
          <p>(4) evidence that the claimant's exposure to the substance in the amount and duration experienced by the claimant could reasonably have been caused or significantly contributed to by the release of a harmful substance from a facility where the substance was placed or came to be located, to the extent the information is available to the claimant;</p>
          <p>(5) evidence that the exposure experienced by the claimant can cause or can significantly contribute to the injury suffered by the claimant;</p>
          <p>(6) evidence of the injury eligible for compensation suffered by the claimant and the compensable losses resulting from the injury;</p>
          <p>(7) evidence of any property damage eligible for compensation and the amount of compensable losses resulting from the damage;</p>
          <p>(8) information regarding any collateral sources of compensation; and</p>
          <p>(9) other information required by the rules of the agency.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115B.32.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Death claims.</span></h2>
          <p>In any case in which death is claimed as a compensable injury, the claim may be brought on behalf of the claimant by the claimant's estate for compensable medical expenses and by the claimant's trustee for death benefits for the claimant's dependents as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/176.111">176.111</a>.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.115B.32.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/1/8/#laws.0.11.0">1Sp1985 c 8 s 11</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/325/#laws.0.78.0">1989 c 325 s 78</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/199/#laws.1.24.0">1991 c 199 art 1 s 24</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2002/0/379/#laws.2.11.0">2002 c 379 art 2 s 11</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/128/#laws.2.21.0">2003 c 128 art 2 s 21</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.115B.33">
        <h1 class="shn">115B.33 CLAIM STANDARDS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115B.33.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Standard for personal injury.</span></h2>
          <p>The agency shall grant compensation to a claimant who shows that it is more likely than not that:</p>
          <p>(1) the claimant suffers a medically verified injury that is eligible for compensation from the fund and that has resulted in a compensable loss;</p>
          <p>(2) the claimant has been exposed to a harmful substance;</p>
          <p>(3) the release of the harmful substance from a facility where the substance was placed or came to be located could reasonably have resulted in the claimant's exposure to the substance in the amount and duration experienced by the claimant; and</p>
          <p>(4) the injury suffered by the claimant can be caused or significantly contributed to by exposure to the harmful substance in an amount and duration experienced by the claimant.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115B.33.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Standard for property damage.</span></h2>
          <p>The agency shall grant compensation to a claimant who shows that it is more likely than not that:</p>
          <p>(1) the claimant has suffered property damage that is eligible for compensation and that has resulted in compensable loss; and</p>
          <p>(2) the presence of the harmful substance in or on the property could reasonably have resulted from the release of the harmful substance from a facility where the substance was placed or came to be located.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.115B.33.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/1/8/#laws.0.12.0">1Sp1985 c 8 s 12</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/325/#laws.0.78.0">1989 c 325 s 78</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/199/#laws.1.25.0">1991 c 199 art 1 s 25</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2002/0/379/#laws.2.12.0">2002 c 379 art 2 s 12</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/128/#laws.2.22.0">2003 c 128 art 2 s 22</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.115B.34">
        <h1 class="shn">115B.34 COMPENSABLE LOSSES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115B.34.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Personal injury losses.</span></h2>
          <p>Losses compensable by the fund for personal injury are limited to:</p>
          <p>(1) medical expenses directly related to the claimant's injury;</p>
          <p>(2) up to two-thirds of the claimant's lost wages not to exceed $2,000 per month or $24,000 per year;</p>
          <p>(3) up to two-thirds of a self-employed claimant's lost income, not to exceed $2,000 per month or $24,000 per year;</p>
          <p>(4) death benefits to dependents which the agency shall define by rule subject to the following conditions:</p>
          <p>(i) the rule adopted by the agency must establish a schedule of benefits similar to that established by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/176.111">176.111</a> and must not provide for the payment of benefits to dependents other than those dependents defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/176.111">176.111</a>;</p>
          <p>(ii) the total benefits paid to all dependents of a claimant must not exceed $2,000 per month;</p>
          <p>(iii) benefits paid to a spouse and all dependents other than children must not continue for a period longer than ten years;</p>
          <p>(iv) payment of benefits is subject to the limitations of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115B.36">115B.36</a>; and</p>
          <p>(5) the value of household labor lost due to the claimant's injury or disease, which must be determined in accordance with a schedule established by the agency by rule, not to exceed $2,000 per month or $24,000 per year.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115B.34.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Property damage losses.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Losses compensable by the fund for property damage are limited to the following losses caused by damage to the principal residence of the claimant:</p>
          <p>(1) the reasonable cost of replacing or decontaminating the primary source of drinking water for the property not to exceed the amount actually expended by the claimant or assessed by a local taxing authority, if the Department of Health has confirmed that the remedy provides safe drinking water and advised that the water not be used for drinking or determined that the replacement or decontamination of the source of drinking water was necessary, up to a maximum of $25,000;</p>
          <p>(2) the reasonable cost to install a mitigation system for the claimant's principal residence, not to exceed the amount actually expended by the claimant, if the agency has recommended such installation to protect human health due to soil vapor intrusion into the residence from releases of harmful substances. Reimbursement of eligible claims shall not exceed $25,000;</p>
          <p>(3) losses incurred as a result of a bona fide sale of the property at less than the appraised market value under circumstances that constitute a hardship to the owner, limited to 75 percent of the difference between the appraised market value and the selling price, but not to exceed $25,000; and</p>
          <p>(4) losses incurred as a result of the inability of an owner in hardship circumstances to sell the property due to the presence of harmful substances, limited to the increase in costs associated with the need to maintain two residences, but not to exceed $25,000.</p>
          <p>(b) In computation of the loss under paragraph (a), clause (4), the agency shall offset the loss by the amount of any income received by the claimant from the rental of the property.</p>
          <p>(c) For purposes of paragraph (a), the following definitions apply:</p>
          <p>(1) "appraised market value" means an appraisal of the market value of the property disregarding any decrease in value caused by the presence of a harmful substance in or on the property; and</p>
          <p>(2) "hardship" means an urgent need to sell the property based on a special circumstance of the owner including catastrophic medical expenses, inability of the owner to physically maintain the property due to a physical or mental condition, and change of employment of the owner or other member of the owner's household requiring the owner to move to a different location.</p>
          <p>(d) Appraisals are subject to agency approval. The agency may adopt rules governing approval of appraisals, criteria for establishing a hardship, and other matters necessary to administer this subdivision.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.115B.34.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/1/8/#laws.0.13.0">1Sp1985 c 8 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/325/#laws.0.45.0">1989 c 325 s 45</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/199/#laws.1.26.0">1991 c 199 art 1 s 26</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2002/0/379/#laws.2.13.0">2002 c 379 art 2 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/128/#laws.2.23.0">2003 c 128 art 2 s 23</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2011/0/76/#laws.1.9.0">2011 c 76 art 1 s 9</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2015/1/4/#laws.4.112.0">1Sp2015 c 4 art 4 s 112</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.115B.35">
        <h1 class="shn">115B.35 DETERMINING CLAIMS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115B.35.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2002/0/379/#laws.2.24.0">2002 c 379 art 2 s 24</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115B.35.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Treatment of protected information.</span></h2>
          <p>In making a final decision under this section, the agency shall examine protected information outside of the presence of the claimant, the claimant's attorney, or any other person except agency staff. The agency, the agency's staff, and any other person who obtains access to protected information under this section may not reveal protected information to any person except as provided in this section.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115B.35.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Evidence admissible in claim proceedings.</span></h2>
          <p>In the determination of a claim, the agency may admit and give probative effect to evidence that possesses probative value commonly accepted by reasonable and prudent persons in the conduct of their affairs. The agency shall give effect to the rules of privilege recognized by law. The agency may exclude incompetent, irrelevant, immaterial, and repetitious evidence.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115B.35.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Preliminary decision.</span></h2>
          <p>The agency shall review all materials filed in support of the claim and may cause an investigation to be conducted into the validity of the claim. The agency may make a preliminary decision on the basis of the papers filed in support of the claim and the report of any investigation of it. The decision must be in writing and include the reasons for the decision, subject to the limitations on disclosure of protected information.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115B.35.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2002/0/379/#laws.2.24.0">2002 c 379 art 2 s 24</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115B.35.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2002/0/379/#laws.2.24.0">2002 c 379 art 2 s 24</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115B.35.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Record.</span></h2>
          <p>Any appearance by a claimant or witnesses must be tape recorded but a formal record pursuant to chapter 14 is not required.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115B.35.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Appeal.</span></h2>
          <p>A final decision of the agency made under this section is conclusive on all matters decided. There is no right to judicial review of a final decision of the agency.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115B.35.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">Remedies and penalties.</span></h2>
          <p>An agency member, agency staff person, or other person who reveals protected information in violation of this section is subject to the civil remedies contained in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/13.08">13.08</a> and the penalties in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/13.09">13.09</a>.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.115B.35.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/1/8/#laws.0.14.0">1Sp1985 c 8 s 14</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2002/0/379/#laws.2.14.0">2002 c 379 art 2 s 14</a>-18</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.115B.36">
        <h1 class="shn">115B.36 AMOUNT AND FORM OF PAYMENT.</h1>
        <p>(a) If the agency decides to grant compensation, it shall determine the net uncompensated loss payable to the claimant by computing the total amount of compensable losses payable to the claimant and subtracting the total amount of any compensation received by the claimant for the same injury or damage from other sources including, but not limited to, all forms of insurance and Social Security and any emergency award made by the agency. The agency shall pay compensation in the amount of the net uncompensated loss, provided that no claimant may receive more than $250,000. In the case of a death, the total amount paid to all persons on behalf of the claimant may not exceed $250,000.</p>
        <p>(b) Compensation from the fund may be awarded in a lump sum or in installments at the discretion of the agency.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.115B.36.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/1/8/#laws.0.15.0">1Sp1985 c 8 s 15</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/199/#laws.1.27.0">1991 c 199 art 1 s 27</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2002/0/379/#laws.2.19.0">2002 c 379 art 2 s 19</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/128/#laws.2.24.0">2003 c 128 art 2 s 24</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.115B.37">
        <h1 class="shn">115B.37 ATTORNEY FEES.</h1>
        <p>The agency may by rule limit the fee charged by any attorney for representing a claimant before the agency.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.115B.37.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/1/8/#laws.0.16.0">1Sp1985 c 8 s 16</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2002/0/379/#laws.2.20.0">2002 c 379 art 2 s 20</a></p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">LANDFILL CLEANUP PROGRAM</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.115B.39">
        <h1 class="shn">115B.39 LANDFILL CLEANUP PROGRAM; ESTABLISHMENT.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115B.39.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Establishment.</span></h2>
          <p>The landfill cleanup program is established to provide environmental response at qualified facilities and is to be administered by the commissioner.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115B.39.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Definitions.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) In addition to the definitions in this subdivision, the definitions in sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.03">115A.03</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115B.02">115B.02</a> apply to sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115B.39">115B.39</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115B.445">115B.445</a>, except as specifically modified in this subdivision.</p>
          <p>(b) "Cleanup order" means a consent order between responsible persons and the agency or an order issued by the United States Environmental Protection Agency under section 106 of the federal Superfund Act.</p>
          <p>(c) "Closure" means actions to prevent or minimize the threat to public health and the environment posed by a mixed municipal solid waste disposal facility that has stopped accepting waste by controlling the sources of releases or threatened releases at the facility. "Closure" includes removing contaminated equipment and liners; applying final cover; grading and seeding final cover; installing wells, borings, and other monitoring devices; constructing groundwater and surface water diversion structures; and installing gas control systems and site security systems, as necessary. The commissioner may authorize use of final cover that includes processed materials that meet the requirements in Code of Federal Regulations, title 40, section 503.32, paragraph (a).</p>
          <p>(d) "Closure upgrade" means construction activity that will, at a minimum, modify an existing cover so that it satisfies current rule requirements for mixed municipal solid waste land disposal facilities.</p>
          <p>(e) "Contingency action" means organized, planned, or coordinated courses of action to be followed in case of fire, explosion, or release of solid waste, waste by-products, or leachate that could threaten human health or the environment.</p>
          <p>(f) "Corrective action" means steps taken to repair facility structures including liners, monitoring wells, separation equipment, covers, and aeration devices and to bring the facility into compliance with design, construction, groundwater, surface water, and air emission standards.</p>
          <p>(g) "Custodial" or "custodial care" means actions taken for the care, maintenance, and monitoring of closure actions at a mixed municipal solid waste disposal facility after completion of the postclosure period.</p>
          <p>(h) "Decomposition gases" means gases produced by chemical or microbial activity during the decomposition of solid waste.</p>
          <p>(i) "Dump materials" means nonhazardous mixed municipal solid wastes disposed at a Minnesota waste disposal site other than a qualified facility prior to 1973.</p>
          <p>(j) "Environmental response action" means response action at a qualified facility or priority qualified facility, including corrective action, closure, postclosure care; contingency action; environmental studies, including remedial investigations and feasibility studies; engineering, including remedial design; removal; remedial action; site construction; and other similar cleanup-related activities.</p>
          <p>(k) "Environmental response costs" means:</p>
          <p>(1) costs of environmental response action, not including legal or administrative expenses; and</p>
          <p>(2) costs required to be paid to the federal government under section 107(a) of the federal Superfund Act, as amended.</p>
          <p>(l) "Owner or operator of a priority qualified facility" means a person, personal representative, trustee, beneficiary, partnership, sole proprietorship, firm, limited liability company, cooperative, association, corporation, or other entity that:</p>
          <p>(1) has possession of, holds title to, or owns a controlling interest in a priority qualified facility;</p>
          <p>(2) participates in decision making related to compliance with federal and state environmental laws and regulations for a priority qualified facility; or</p>
          <p>(3) has authority or control to make decisions regarding state and federal environmental laws and regulations for a priority qualified facility.</p>
          <p>(m) "Priority qualified facility" means:</p>
          <p>(1) a qualified facility:</p>
          <p>(i) that is listed on the National Priorities List pursuant to the federal Comprehensive Environmental Response, Compensation, and Liability Act;</p>
          <p>(ii) that is listed on the Permanent List of Priorities pursuant to the Minnesota Environmental Response and Liability Act;</p>
          <p>(iii) for which a binding agreement pursuant to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115B.40#stat.115B.40.4">115B.40, subdivision 4</a>, has not been entered into between the owner or operator of the qualified facility and the commissioner; and</p>
          <p>(iv) that is not an excluded facility pursuant to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115B.405">115B.405</a>; and</p>
          <p>(2) property located within 750 feet from the boundary of a facility described in clause (1), including any contiguous property:</p>
          <p>(i) that is listed on the Permanent List of Priorities pursuant to the Minnesota Environmental Response and Liability Act, as of May 31, 2017;</p>
          <p>(ii) where mixed municipal solid waste was disposed of within the boundaries of the property, which disposal did not occur under a permit from the agency; and</p>
          <p>(iii) for which the commissioner determines an environmental response action is necessary to protect public health or welfare or the environment at and in the vicinity of the facility described in clause (1).</p>
          <p>(n) "Postclosure" or "postclosure care" means actions taken for the care, maintenance, and monitoring of closure actions at a mixed municipal solid waste disposal facility.</p>
          <p>(o) "Qualified facility" means a mixed municipal solid waste disposal facility as described in the most recent agency permit, including adjacent property used for solid waste disposal that did not occur under a permit from the agency, that:</p>
          <p>(1)(i) is or was permitted by the agency;</p>
          <p>(ii) stopped accepting solid waste, except demolition debris, for disposal by April 9, 1994; and</p>
          <p>(iii) stopped accepting demolition debris for disposal by June 1, 1994, except that demolition debris may be accepted until May 1, 1995, at a permitted area where disposal of demolition debris is allowed, if the area where the demolition debris is deposited is at least 50 feet from the fill boundary of the area where mixed municipal solid waste was deposited;</p>
          <p>(2) is or was permitted by the agency and</p>
          <p>(i) stopped accepting waste by January 1, 2000, except that demolition debris, industrial waste, and municipal solid waste combustor ash may be accepted until January 1, 2001, at a permitted area where disposal of such waste is allowed, if the area where the waste is deposited is at least 50 feet from the fill boundary of the area where mixed municipal solid waste was deposited; or</p>
          <p>(ii) stopped accepting waste by January 1, 2019, and is located in a county that meets all applicable recycling goals in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.551">115A.551</a> and that has arranged for all mixed municipal solid waste generated in the county to be delivered to and processed by a resource recovery facility located in the county for at least 20 years; or</p>
          <p>(3) is or was permitted by the agency and stopped accepting waste for disposal by January 1, 2009, and for which the postclosure care period ended on July 26, 2013.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.115B.39.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/639/#laws.1.2.0">1994 c 639 art 1 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/7/#laws.1.33.0">1997 c 7 art 1 s 33</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/306/#laws.0.1.0">1998 c 306 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/231/#laws.0.133.0">1999 c 231 s 133</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/289/#laws.0.59.0">2014 c 289 s 59</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/0/93/#laws.2.124.0">2017 c 93 art 2 s 124</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.115B.40">
        <h1 class="shn">115B.40 PROGRAM.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115B.40.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Response to releases.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner may take any environmental response action, including emergency action, related to a release or threatened release of a hazardous substance, pollutant or contaminant, or decomposition gas from a qualified facility that the commissioner deems reasonable and necessary to protect the public health or welfare or the environment under the standards required in sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115B.01">115B.01</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115B.20">115B.20</a>. The commissioner may undertake studies necessary to determine reasonable and necessary environmental response actions at individual facilities. The commissioner may develop general work plans for environmental studies, presumptive remedies, and generic remedial designs for facilities with similar characteristics. Prior to selecting environmental response actions for a facility, the commissioner shall hold at least one public informational meeting near the facility and provide for receiving and responding to comments related to the selection. The commissioner shall design, implement, and provide oversight consistent with the actions selected under this subdivision.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115B.40.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Priority list.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The commissioner shall establish a priority list for preventing or responding to releases of hazardous substances, pollutants and contaminants, or decomposition gases at qualified facilities defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115B.39#stat.115B.39.2">115B.39, subdivision 2</a>, paragraph (o), clause (1). The commissioner shall periodically revise the list to reflect changing conditions at facilities that affect priority for response actions. The initial priority list must be established by January 1, 1995.</p>
          <p>(b) The priority list required under this subdivision must be based on the relative risk or danger to public health or welfare or the environment, taking into account to the extent possible the population at risk, the hazardous potential of the hazardous substances at the facility, the potential for contamination of drinking water supplies, the potential for direct human contact, and the potential for destruction of sensitive ecosystems.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115B.40.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Notification.</span></h2>
          <p>By September 1, 1994, the commissioner shall notify the owner or operator of, and persons subject to a cleanup order at, each qualified facility defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115B.39#stat.115B.39.2">115B.39, subdivision 2</a>, paragraph (o), clause (1), of whether the requirements of subdivision 4 or 5 have been met. If the requirements have not been met at a facility, the commissioner, by the earliest practicable date, shall notify the owner or operator and persons subject to a cleanup order of what actions need to be taken.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115B.40.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Qualified facility not under cleanup order; duties.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The owner or operator of a qualified facility that is not subject to a cleanup order shall:</p>
          <p>(1) complete closure activities at the facility, or enter into a binding agreement with the commissioner to do so, as provided in paragraph (e), within one year from the date the owner or operator is notified by the commissioner under subdivision 3 of the closure activities that are necessary to properly close the facility in compliance with facility's permit, closure orders, or enforcement agreement with the agency, and with the solid waste rules in effect at the time the facility stopped accepting waste;</p>
          <p>(2) undertake or continue postclosure and custodial care at the facility until the date of notice of compliance under subdivision 7;</p>
          <p>(3) in the case of qualified facilities defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115B.39#stat.115B.39.2">115B.39, subdivision 2</a>, paragraph (o), clause (1), transfer to the commissioner of revenue for deposit in the remediation fund established in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.155">116.155</a> any funds required for proof of financial responsibility under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.07#stat.116.07.4h">116.07, subdivision 4h</a>, that remain after facility closure and any postclosure care and response action undertaken by the owner or operator at the facility including, if proof of financial responsibility is provided through a letter of credit or other financial instrument or mechanism that does not accumulate money in an account, the amount that would have accumulated had the owner or operator utilized a trust fund, less any amount used for closure, postclosure care, and response action at the facility;</p>
          <p>(4) in the case of qualified facilities defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115B.39#stat.115B.39.2">115B.39, subdivision 2</a>, paragraph (o), clause (2), transfer to the commissioner of revenue for deposit in the remediation fund established in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.155">116.155</a> an amount of cash that is equal to the sum of their approved current contingency action cost estimate and the present value of their approved estimated remaining postclosure care costs required for proof of financial responsibility under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.07#stat.116.07.4h">116.07, subdivision 4h</a>; and</p>
          <p>(5) in the case of qualified facilities defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115B.39#stat.115B.39.2">115B.39, subdivision 2</a>, paragraph (o), clause (3), transfer to the commissioner of revenue for deposit in the remediation fund established in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.155">116.155</a> an amount of cash that is equal to the sum of their approved current contingency action cost estimate and any funds required for proof of financial responsibility under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.07#stat.116.07.4h">116.07, subdivision 4h</a>, that remain after facility closure and any postclosure and custodial care and response action undertaken by the owner or operator at the facility.</p>
          <p>(b) The owner or operator of a qualified facility that is not subject to a cleanup order shall:</p>
          <p>(1) in the case of qualified facilities defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115B.39#stat.115B.39.2">115B.39, subdivision 2</a>, paragraph (o), clause (1), provide the commissioner with a copy of all applicable comprehensive general liability insurance policies and other liability policies relating to property damage, certificates, or other evidence of insurance coverage held during the life of the facility; and</p>
          <p>(2) enter into a binding agreement with the commissioner to:</p>
          <p>(i) in the case of qualified facilities defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115B.39#stat.115B.39.2">115B.39, subdivision 2</a>, paragraph (o), clause (1), take any actions necessary to preserve the owner or operator's rights to payment or defense under insurance policies included in clause (1); cooperate with the commissioner in asserting claims under the policies; and, within 60 days of a request by the commissioner, but no earlier than July 1, 1996, assign only those rights under the policies related to environmental response costs;</p>
          <p>(ii) cooperate with the commissioner or other persons acting at the direction of the commissioner in taking additional environmental response actions necessary to address releases or threatened releases and to avoid any action that interferes with environmental response actions, including allowing entry to the property and to the facility's records and allowing entry and installation of equipment; and</p>
          <p>(iii) refrain from developing or altering the use of property described in any permit for the facility except after consultation with the commissioner and in conformance with any conditions established by the commissioner for that property, including use restrictions, to protect public health and welfare and the environment.</p>
          <p>(c) The owner or operator of a qualified facility defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115B.39#stat.115B.39.2">115B.39, subdivision 2</a>, paragraph (o), clause (1), that is a political subdivision may use a portion of any funds established for response at the facility, which are available directly or through a financial instrument or other financial arrangement, for closure or postclosure care at the facility if funds available for closure or postclosure care are inadequate and shall assign the rights to any remainder to the commissioner.</p>
          <p>(d) The agreement required in paragraph (b), clause (2), must be in writing and must apply to and be binding upon the successors and assigns of the owner. The owner shall record the agreement, or a memorandum approved by the commissioner that summarizes the agreement, with the county recorder or registrar of titles of the county where the property is located.</p>
          <p>(e) A binding agreement entered into under paragraph (a), clause (1), may include a provision that the owner or operator will reimburse the commissioner for the costs of closing the facility to the standard required in that clause.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115B.40.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Qualified facility under cleanup order; duties.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) For a qualified facility that is subject to a cleanup order, persons identified in the order shall complete construction of the remedy required under the cleanup order and:</p>
          <p>(1) for a federal order, receive a concurrent determination of the United States Environmental Protection Agency and the agency or commissioner that the remedy is functioning properly and is performing as designed; or</p>
          <p>(2) for a state order, receive acknowledgment from the agency or commissioner that the obligations under the order for construction of the remedy have been met.</p>
          <p>(b) The owner or operator of a qualified facility that is subject to a cleanup order, in addition to any applicable requirement in paragraph (a), shall comply with subdivision 4, paragraphs (a), clause (3) or (4); and (b).</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115B.40.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Commissioner; duties.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) If the owner or operator of a qualified facility that is subject to the requirements of subdivision 4, paragraph (a), fails to comply with subdivision 4, paragraph (a), clause (1) or (2), the commissioner shall:</p>
          <p>(1) undertake or complete closure activities at the facility in compliance with the solid waste rules in effect at the time the commissioner takes action under this clause; and</p>
          <p>(2) undertake or continue postclosure care at the facility as required under subdivision 2.</p>
          <p>(b) If a facility has been properly closed under subdivision 4, but the applicable closure requirements are less environmentally protective than closure requirements in the solid waste rules in effect on January 1, 1993, the commissioner shall determine whether the facility should be closed to the higher standards and, if so, shall undertake additional closure activities at the facility to meet those standards. The commissioner may determine that additional closure activities are unnecessary only if it is likely that response actions will be taken in the near future and that those response actions will result in removal or significant alteration of the closure activities or render the closure activities unnecessary.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115B.40.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Notice of compliance; effects.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The commissioner shall provide written notice of compliance to the appropriate owner or operator or person subject to a cleanup order when:</p>
          <p>(1) the commissioner determines that the requirements of subdivision 4 or 5 have been met; and</p>
          <p>(2) the person who will receive the notice has submitted to the commissioner a written waiver of any claims the person may have against any other person for recovery of any environmental response costs related to a qualified facility that were incurred prior to the date of notice of compliance.</p>
          <p>(b) Beginning on the date of the notice of compliance:</p>
          <p>(1) the commissioner shall assume all obligations of the owner or operator or person for environmental response actions under the federal Superfund Act and any federal or state cleanup orders and shall undertake all further action under subdivision 1 at or related to the facility that the commissioner deems appropriate and in accordance with the priority list; and</p>
          <p>(2) the commissioner may not seek recovery against the owner or operator of the facility or any responsible person of any costs incurred by the commissioner for environmental response action at or related to the facility, except:</p>
          <p>(i) in the case of qualified facilities defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115B.39#stat.115B.39.2">115B.39, subdivision 2</a>, paragraph (o), clause (1), to the extent of insurance coverage held by the owner or operator or responsible person; or</p>
          <p>(ii) as provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115B.402">115B.402</a>.</p>
          <p>(c) The commissioner and the attorney general shall communicate with the United States Environmental Protection Agency addressing the manner and procedure for the state's assumption of federal obligations under paragraph (b), clause (1).</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115B.40.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Statutes of limitations.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) With respect to claims for recovery of environmental response costs related to qualified facilities defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115B.39#stat.115B.39.2">115B.39, subdivision 2</a>, paragraph (o), clause (1), the running of all applicable periods of limitation under state law is suspended until July 1, 2004.</p>
          <p>(b) A waiver of claims for recovery of environmental response costs under this section or section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115B.43">115B.43</a> is extinguished for that portion of reimbursable costs under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115B.43">115B.43</a> that have not been reimbursed by July 1, 2004.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.115B.40.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/639/#laws.1.3.0">1994 c 639 art 1 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/220/#laws.0.130.0">1995 c 220 s 130</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/231/#laws.0.134.0">1999 c 231 s 134</a>-140; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/128/#laws.2.25.0">2003 c 128 art 2 s 25</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2004/0/228/#laws.1.73.0">2004 c 228 art 1 s 73</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/0/93/#laws.2.125.0">2017 c 93 art 2 s 125</a>,165</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.115B.402">
        <h1 class="shn">115B.402 ILLEGAL ACTIONS AT QUALIFIED FACILITIES.</h1>
        <p>The commissioner may recover under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115B.17#stat.115B.17.6">115B.17, subdivision 6</a>, that portion of the costs of response actions at any qualified facility attributable to a person who otherwise would be responsible for the release or threatened release under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115B.03">115B.03</a>, and whose actions related to the release or threatened release were in violation of federal or state hazardous waste management laws in effect at the time of those actions. The commissioner's determination of the portion of the costs of a response action attributable to a person under this section, based on the volume and toxicity of waste in the facility associated with the person and other factors reasonably related to the contribution of the person to a release or threatened release, is prima facie evidence that those costs are attributable to the person.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.115B.402.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/639/#laws.1.4.0">1994 c 639 art 1 s 4</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.115B.403">
        <h1 class="shn">115B.403 ACCEPTING DUMP MATERIALS TO AUGMENT CLOSURE OR CLOSURE UPGRADE ACTIVITIES AT QUALIFIED FACILITIES.</h1>
        <p>(a) The commissioner may allow dump materials at qualified facilities from sites that have been in public ownership on or after January 1, 1998, provided that the commissioner finds that:</p>
        <p>(1) accepting the dump materials will not significantly increase the potential environmental impacts of the qualified facility;</p>
        <p>(2) accepting the dump materials will benefit the qualified facility by improving final slopes, or in some other tangible manner, and the materials will be provided to the qualified facility according to a schedule consistent with closure or closure upgrade activities at the qualified facility;</p>
        <p>(3) the owner of the site where the dump materials were disposed complies with the requirements of paragraph (b) and agrees to:</p>
        <p>(i) pay any costs of screening and inspection needed at point of excavation or point of deposit to ensure that unacceptable materials are not delivered to the qualified facility;</p>
        <p>(ii) secure any permissions necessary from the owner of the property containing the qualified facility;</p>
        <p>(iii) transport the dump materials to the qualified facility according to a process and schedule acceptable to the commissioner and, at owner's cost, pay all transportation costs related to the activity; and</p>
        <p>(iv) complete cleanup, final grading, seeding, and other related activities for the area where the dump existed.</p>
        <p>(b) In order to qualify for consideration for disposal of dump materials at qualified facilities, the owner of the site where the dump materials were disposed shall notify the commissioner, in writing, at least six months before closure activity at the qualified facility indicating the owner's desire to dispose of the dump material at the qualified facility in order to allow adequate time for construction planning and public information activities. The commissioner shall consider requests based on the schedule of closure and closure upgrade activities at qualified facilities. The commissioner shall require the owner of the site to submit, at the owner's expense, physical and chemical evaluation data regarding the dump material including any necessary coring, excavation, or other sampling data at least six months before closure construction to assist the commissioner in making the determination whether or not to accept the material.</p>
        <p>(c) The commissioner may reject any and all requests to dispose of dump materials at qualified facilities.</p>
        <p>(d) Nothing in this section shall be construed as allowing unpermitted disposal facilities to be included as qualified facilities in the closed landfill program.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.115B.403.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/306/#laws.0.2.0">1998 c 306 s 2</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.115B.405">
        <h1 class="shn">115B.405 EXCLUDED FACILITIES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115B.405.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Application.</span></h2>
          <p>The owner or operator of a qualified facility may apply to the commissioner for exclusion from the landfill cleanup program under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115B.39">115B.39</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115B.40">115B.40</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115B.41">115B.41</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115B.412">115B.412</a>, and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115B.43">115B.43</a>. Applications for qualified facilities defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115B.39#stat.115B.39.2">115B.39, subdivision 2</a>, paragraph (o), clause (1), must be received by the commissioner by February 1, 1995. Applications for qualified facilities defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115B.39#stat.115B.39.2">115B.39, subdivision 2</a>, paragraph (o), clause (2), must be received by the commissioner by December 31, 1999. The owner or operator of a qualified facility that is subject to a federal cleanup order or that includes any portion that is tax-forfeited may not apply for exclusion under this section. In addition to other information required by the commissioner, an application must include a disclosure of all financial assurance accounts established for the facility. Applications for exclusion must:</p>
          <p>(1) show that the operator or owner is complying with the agency's rules adopted under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.07#stat.116.07.4h">116.07, subdivision 4h</a>, and is complying with a financial assurance plan for the facility that the commissioner has approved after determining that the plan is adequate to provide for closure, postclosure care, and contingency action;</p>
          <p>(2) demonstrate that the facility is closed or is in compliance with a closure schedule approved by the commissioner; and</p>
          <p>(3) include a waiver of all claims for recovery of costs incurred under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115B.01">115B.01</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115B.20">115B.20</a> and the federal Superfund Act at or related to a qualified facility.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115B.405.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Exclusion status; notification.</span></h2>
          <p>Within 90 days after the commissioner has received a complete application for exclusion, the commissioner shall notify the owner or operator if the facility is excluded. If the commissioner finds that the facility does not satisfy the requirements for exclusion, the commissioner shall notify the owner or operator of that fact.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115B.405.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Restriction on using property at excluded facilities.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A person may not use any property described in the most recent agency permit issued for an excluded facility in any way that disturbs the integrity of the final cover, liners, or any other components of any containment system, or the function of the facility's monitoring systems, unless the agency finds that the disturbance:</p>
          <p>(1) is necessary to the proposed use of the property and will not increase the potential hazard to human health or the environment; or</p>
          <p>(2) is necessary to reduce a threat to human health or the environment.</p>
          <p>(b) Before any transfer of ownership of property described in paragraph (a), the owner must obtain approval from the commissioner. The commissioner shall approve a transfer if the owner can demonstrate to the satisfaction of the commissioner that persons and property will not be exposed to undue risk from releases of hazardous substances or pollutants or contaminants.</p>
          <p>(c) After obtaining approval from the commissioner, the owner shall record with the county recorder of the county in which the property is located an affidavit containing a legal description of the property that discloses to any potential transferee:</p>
          <p>(1) that the land has been used as a mixed municipal solid waste disposal facility;</p>
          <p>(2) the identity, quantity, location, condition, and circumstances of the disposal and any release of hazardous substances or pollutants or contaminants from the facility to the full extent known or reasonably ascertainable; and</p>
          <p>(3) that the use of the property or some portion of it may be restricted as provided in paragraph (a).</p>
          <p>(d) An owner must also file an affidavit within 60 days after any material change in any matter required to be disclosed under paragraph (c), with respect to property for which an affidavit has already been recorded. If the owner or any subsequent owner of the property removes the waste from the facility together with any residues, liner, and contaminated underlying and surrounding soil, that owner may record an affidavit indicating the removal. Failure to record an affidavit as provided in this paragraph does not affect or prevent any transfer of ownership of the property.</p>
          <p>(e) The county recorder shall record all affidavits presented in accordance with paragraphs (c) and (d). The affidavits must be recorded in a manner that will ensure their disclosure in the ordinary course of a title search of the subject property.</p>
          <p>(f) This subdivision is enforceable under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115.071">115.071</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.072">116.072</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115B.405.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Closure.</span></h2>
          <p>If the commissioner determines that the owner or operator of an excluded facility did not complete the terms of an approved closure plan by the date in the plan, the commissioner shall complete closure at the facility and may seek cost recovery against the owner or operator under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115B.17#stat.115B.17.6">115B.17, subdivision 6</a>.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.115B.405.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/639/#laws.1.5.0">1994 c 639 art 1 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/231/#laws.0.141.0">1999 c 231 s 141</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2004/0/228/#laws.1.73.0">2004 c 228 art 1 s 73</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/0/93/#laws.2.165.0">2017 c 93 art 2 s 165</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.115B.406">
        <h1 class="shn">115B.406 PRIORITY QUALIFIED FACILITIES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115B.406.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Legislative findings.</span></h2>
          <p>The legislature recognizes the need to protect the public health and welfare and the environment at priority qualified facilities. To implement a timely and effective cleanup and prevent multiparty litigation, the legislature finds it is in the public interest to direct the commissioner of the Pollution Control Agency to:</p>
          <p>(1) take environmental response actions that the commissioner deems reasonable and necessary to protect the public health or welfare or the environment at priority qualified facilities;</p>
          <p>(2) acquire real property interests at priority qualified facilities to ensure the completion and long-term effectiveness of environmental response actions; and</p>
          <p>(3) prevent both an unjust financial windfall to and double liability of owners and operators of priority qualified facilities.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115B.406.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Notifying owner or operator of priority qualified facility.</span></h2>
          <p>Within 30 days after May 31, 2017, or within 30 days after section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115B.39">115B.39, subdivision </a>2, paragraph (m), applies to a facility, whichever is later, the commissioner must notify the owner or operator of a qualified facility that the facility is a priority qualified facility under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115B.39#stat.115B.39.2">115B.39, subdivision 2</a>, paragraph (m). Within 60 days after being notified under this subdivision, the owner or operator of a priority qualified facility must enter into a binding agreement with the commissioner according to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115B.40#stat.115B.40.4">115B.40, subdivision 4</a>, paragraph (b).</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115B.406.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">State response.</span></h2>
